I don't really get a break from it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's severe. It's not just a little sadness in the background... it's heavy enough that it drags down everything I try to do. It takes a lot out of me just to get through a normal day with it weighing on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed either of those, to be honest. I mean, I feel heavy, like I mentioned, but I don't think I'm actually moving or talking in slow motion or anything like that. And I'm definitely not restless. I know I just said I don't see things getting better, but... I don't want to hurt myself or anything like that. I'm just incredibly tired of feeling this way, day in and day out. But no, I haven't had any thoughts about dying.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since I took on the mortgage for the studio. That was a little over two years ago... probably about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My appetite is pretty much the same... I still eat normally. And my sleep... I mean, I'm always tired, like I said, but I'm sleeping about the same amount I always have at night. I haven't noticed any big changes with either of those.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most things, or is it more that you just don't feel like doing them because they feel pointless?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a bit of both, really. Most days I just wake up with this... heavy, gray feeling already sitting on me. It's like I can't shake it. And even when I'm out in the shop, surrounded by the wood I used to love working with, it just all feels flat. I have to fight the urge to just stand there staring at it, because doing anything with it just feels pointless. So yeah, I guess the interest is gone because everything just feels so heavy.\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel this heavy, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how to count it. I haven't really kept track, to be honest. It's just... pretty much every day. Like I said, I wake up with it, so it just feels like it's always there. I don't really get a break from it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's severe. It's not just a little sadness in the background... it's heavy enough that it drags down everything I try to do. It takes a lot out of me just to get through a normal day with it weighing on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed either of those, to be honest. I mean, I feel heavy, like I mentioned, but I don't think I'm actually moving or talking in slow motion or anything like that. And I'm definitely not restless. I don't have any trouble sitting still... it's more just that I don't have the drive to get up and do things. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm dealing with either of those.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this severe heavy, gray feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since I took on the studio mortgage. That was... a little over two years ago. Yeah, I'd say about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in, besides carpentry?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Carpentry was... well, it was pretty much my whole life. I didn't really have a lot of hobbies outside of the shop. I mean, I'd watch TV or just relax at home, but I never had any other big passions to begin with. So, besides the woodworking... no, I can't really think of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, it was always just the shop for me. Now that that's gone... I don't really do much of anything that I'd say I enjoy. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I honestly just can't see my life getting back on track anymore. I have this carpentry studio, and... no matter how hard I work, or what kind of payment plan they put in front of me, it just feels pointless. I don't see things getting better.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like working with your carpentry studio?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Most days, I wake up and... well, there's just this heavy, gray feeling already sitting on me. Even when I'm out in the shop, surrounded by the wood I used to love working with, it all just feels flat now. Honestly, I have to fight with myself just to not stand there staring into space.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you normally would, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: I... I think I kind of just mentioned that with the shop, but yeah, it's definitely changed. Like I said, I just don't get any enjoyment out of the carpentry anymore, and that used to be my main thing. There's really nothing else I do for fun these days. It all just feels like a chore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your life will ever improve?\n\nPatient: Like I said when I first sat down... no, I really don't. I just can't see my life or the studio ever getting back on track. It doesn't seem to matter how hard I work, or... or what kind of payment plan they put in front of me. I just don't see any of it actually getting better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I know I just said I don't see things getting better, but... I don't want to hurt myself or anything like that. I'm just incredibly tired of feeling this way, day in and day out. But no, I haven't had any thoughts about dying.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since I took on the mortgage for the studio. That was a little over two years ago... probably about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My appetite is pretty much the same... I still eat normally. And my sleep... I mean, I'm always tired, like I said, but I'm sleeping about the same amount I always have at night. I haven't noticed any big changes with either of those.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most things, or is it more that you just don't feel like doing them because they feel pointless?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a bit of both, really. Most days I just wake up with this... heavy, gray feeling already sitting on me. It's like I can't shake it. And even when I'm out in the shop, surrounded by the wood I used to love working with, it just all feels flat. I have to fight the urge to just stand there staring at it, because doing anything with it just feels pointless. So yeah, I guess the interest is gone because everything just feels so heavy.\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel this heavy, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how to count it. I haven't really kept track, to be honest. It's just... pretty much every day. Like I said, I wake up with it, so it just feels like it's always there. I don't really get a break from it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's severe. It's not just a little sadness in the background... it's heavy enough that it drags down everything I try to do. It takes a lot out of me just to get through a normal day with it weighing on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed either of those, to be honest. I mean, I feel heavy, like I mentioned, but I don't think I'm actually moving or talking in slow motion or anything like that. And I'm definitely not restless. I don't have any trouble sitting still... it's more just that I don't have the drive to get up and do things. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm dealing with either of those.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this severe heavy, gray feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since I took on the studio mortgage. Like I just said, there really isn't anything. Even the small stuff... it's just flat. I don't get any enjoyment out of anything right now. I just wake up and wait for the day to be over.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity, no matter how small, that gives you even a slight bit of pleasure?\n\nPatient: Look, doc, I know you keep asking, but my answer is still no. I have this carpentry studio, and... no matter how hard I work, or what kind of payment plan they put in front of me, it just feels pointless. I don't see things getting better.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like working with your carpentry studio?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Most days, I wake up and... well, there's just this heavy, gray feeling already sitting on me. Even when I'm out in the shop, surrounded by the wood I used to love working with, it all just feels flat now. Honestly, I have to fight with myself just to not stand there staring into space.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you normally would, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: I... I think I kind of just mentioned that with the shop, but yeah, it's definitely changed. Like I said, I just don't get any enjoyment out of the carpentry anymore, and that used to be my main thing. There's really nothing else I do for fun these days. It all just feels like a chore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your life will ever improve?\n\nPatient: Like I said when I first sat down... no, I really don't. I just can't see my life or the studio ever getting back on track. It doesn't seem to matter how hard I work, or... or what kind of payment plan they put in front of me. I just don't see any of it actually getting better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I know I just said I don't see things getting better, but... I don't want to hurt myself or anything like that. I'm just incredibly tired of feeling this way, day in and day out. But no, I haven't had any thoughts about dying.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since I took on the mortgage for the studio. That was a little over two years ago... probably about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My appetite is pretty much the same... I still eat normally. And my sleep... I mean, I'm always tired, like I said, but I'm sleeping about the same amount I always have at night. I haven't noticed any big changes with either of those.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most things, or is it more that you just don't feel like doing them because they feel pointless?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a bit of both, really. Most days I just wake up with this... heavy, gray feeling already sitting on me. It's like I can't shake it. And even when I'm out in the shop, surrounded by the wood I used to love working with, it just all feels flat. I have to fight the urge to just stand there staring at it, because doing anything with it just feels pointless. So yeah, I guess the interest is gone because everything just feels so heavy.\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel this heavy, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how to count it. I haven't really kept track, to be honest. It's just... pretty much every day. Like I said, I wake up with it, so it just feels like it's always there. I don't really get a break from it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's severe. It's not just a little sadness in the background... it's heavy enough that it drags down everything I try to do. It takes a lot out of me just to get through a normal day with it weighing on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed either of those, to be honest. I mean, I feel heavy, like I mentioned, but I don't think I'm actually moving or talking in slow motion or anything like that. And I'm definitely not restless. I don't have any trouble sitting still... it's more just that I don't have the drive to get up and do things. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm dealing with either of those.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this severe heavy, gray feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since I took on the studio mortgage. That was... a little over two years ago. Yeah, I'd say about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in, besides carpentry?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Carpentry was... well, it was pretty much my whole life. I didn't really have a lot of hobbies outside of the shop. I mean, I'd watch TV or just relax at home, but I never had any other big passions to begin with. So, besides the woodworking... no, I can't really think of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, it was always just the shop for me. Now that that's gone... I don't really do much of anything that I'd say I enjoy. I just kind of get through the days.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. Like I just said, there really isn't anything. Even the small stuff... it's just flat. I don't get any enjoyment out of anything right now. I just wake up and wait for the day to be over.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity, no matter how small, that gives you even a slight bit of pleasure?\n\nPatient: Look, doc, I know you keep asking, but my answer is still no. There really isn't anything at all. I'm not trying to be difficult, it's just... I don't feel any spark from anything anymore, no matter how small it is. It's just all empty.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find pleasurable, but now find no pleasure in, that you've not mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I wasn't really the type of guy to have a bunch of different hobbies. It was pretty much just my carpentry... being out in the shop. I didn't really do much else besides that, so... no, there's nothing else I can think of.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That was a little over two years ago... probably about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My appetite is pretty much the same... I still eat normally. And my sleep... I mean, I'm always tired, like I said, but I'm sleeping about the same amount I always have at night. I haven't noticed any big changes with either of those.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most things, or is it more that you just don't feel like doing them because they feel pointless?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a bit of both, really. Most days I just wake up with this... heavy, gray feeling already sitting on me. It's like I can't shake it. And even when I'm out in the shop, surrounded by the wood I used to love working with, it just all feels flat. I have to fight the urge to just stand there staring at it, because doing anything with it just feels pointless. So yeah, I guess the interest is gone because everything just feels so heavy.\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel this heavy, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how to count it. I haven't really kept track, to be honest. It's just... pretty much every day. Like I said, I wake up with it, so it just feels like it's always there. I don't really get a break from it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's severe. It's not just a little sadness in the background... it's heavy enough that it drags down everything I try to do. It takes a lot out of me just to get through a normal day with it weighing on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed either of those, to be honest. I mean, I feel heavy, like I mentioned, but I don't think I'm actually moving or talking in slow motion or anything like that. And I'm definitely not restless. I don't have any trouble sitting still... it's more just that I don't have the drive to get up and do things. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm dealing with either of those.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this severe heavy, gray feeling that you described?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since I took on the studio mortgage. That was... a little over two years ago. Yeah, I'd say about two years and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in, besides carpentry?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Carpentry was... well, it was pretty much my whole life. I didn't really have a lot of hobbies outside of the shop. I mean, I'd watch TV or just relax at home, but I never had any other big passions to begin with. So, besides the woodworking... no, I can't really think of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, it was always just the shop for me. Now that that's gone... I don't really do much of anything that I'd say I enjoy. I just kind of get through the days.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. Like I just said, there really isn't anything. Even the small stuff... it's just flat. I don't get any enjoyment out of anything right now. I just wake up and wait for the day to be over.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity, no matter how small, that gives you even a slight bit of pleasure?\n\nPatient: Look, doc, I know you keep asking, but my answer is still no. There really isn't anything at all. I'm not trying to be difficult, it's just... I don't feel any spark from anything anymore, no matter how small it is. It's just all empty.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find pleasurable, but now find no pleasure in, that you've not mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I wasn't really the type of guy to have a bunch of different hobbies. It was pretty much just my carpentry... being out in the shop. I didn't really do much else besides that, so... no, there's nothing else I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to think clearly and make decisions as quickly as you used to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thinking feels... I don't know, just kind of slow and foggy. It takes me a lot longer to figure things out, even simple stuff. And making decisions? Forget it. Even trying to decide what to eat or what to do next just feels like this huge effort. I just can't seem to focus or get my brain to work like it used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. Carpentry was... well, it was pretty much my whole life. I didn't really have a lot of hobbies outside of the shop. I mean, I'd watch TV or just relax at home, but I never had any other big passions to begin with. So, besides the woodworking... no, I can't really think of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, it was always just the shop for me. Now that that's gone... I don't really do much of anything that I'd say I enjoy. I just kind of get through the days.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. Like I just said, there really isn't anything. Even the small stuff... it's just flat. I don't get any enjoyment out of anything right now. I just wake up and wait for the day to be over.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity, no matter how small, that gives you even a slight bit of pleasure?\n\nPatient: Look, doc, I know you keep asking, but my answer is still no. There really isn't anything at all. I'm not trying to be difficult, it's just... I don't feel any spark from anything anymore, no matter how small it is. It's just all empty.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find pleasurable, but now find no pleasure in, that you've not mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I wasn't really the type of guy to have a bunch of different hobbies. It was pretty much just my carpentry... being out in the shop. I didn't really do much else besides that, so... no, there's nothing else I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to think clearly and make decisions as quickly as you used to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thinking feels... I don't know, just kind of slow and foggy. It takes me a lot longer to figure things out, even simple stuff. And making decisions? Forget it. Even trying to decide what to eat or what to do next just feels like this huge effort. I just can't seem to focus or get my brain to work like it used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, my brain feels slow, like I said, but physically? No. I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I feel down and tired all the time, but... no, I've never actually wished I was dead or anything like that. I just want to feel like myself again, you know? But no thoughts like that."
